"et fut Tallebot prisonnier des archers de Poton" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Gruel, p. 319, primary) "was taken prisoner by the Captains La Hire and Poton" (archive.org-download-thelifeofjoanofa19488gut-19488.txt.txt, vol. 1 ch. 16, scholarly)
- 040.33 Event: Fastolf, who had not dismounted, fled the field with a small company and reached Étampes about midnight, then Corbeil. agreed. "Si prinst son chemin vers Estampes" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Wavrin, p. 424, primary) "messire Jehan Fascot, qui se sauva dedans Corbueil" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 178, primary) "messire Jehan Fastoli s'enfuit" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Gruel, p. 319, primary)
- 040.34 Event: Bedford reproached Fastolf for his flight and took the Garter from him, which he later regained. single. Monstrelet. "and he was deprived of the order of the Garter" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 61, primary)
- 040.35 Figure: the English losses at Patay, by author: Monstrelet about 1,800 dead and 100 to 120 taken; Wavrin about 2,000 dead and 200 taken; Gruel 2,200 dead by the heralds' count; the Journal about 2,200 dead, English and French of their party; Cagny 2,000 to 3,000 dead and 400 to 500 taken besides the lords; Dunois more than 4,000 killed and taken; Oman a third or more of the army; France 2,000 dead and 1,200 to 1,500 prisoners. disputed. "About eighteen hundred English were left dead on the field, and from one hundred to six score made prisoners" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 61, primary) "bien deux mille hommes et deux cens prison- niers" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Wavrin, p. 423, primary) "Si furent là morts bien deux mille et deux cents, ainsi que di- soient les héraults" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Gruel, p. 319, primary) "environ deux mil et deux cens, tant Angloys que faulx Françoys" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 177, primary) "A laquelle bataille fuient mis a mort de n à m mille Englois" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 15, primary) "et de iih à v cens autres Englois" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 16, primary) "and of killed and prisoners there were more than 4,000" (archive.org-download-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-djvu.txt.txt, Dunois's deposition, primary) "Talbot was taken prisoner, a third or more of his army was destroyed" (archive.org-download-historyofartofwa0002char-historyofartofwa0002char-djvu.txt.txt, book 14 ch. 2, scholarly) "In all, there were between twelve and fifteen hundred prisoners" (archive.org-download-thelifeofjoanofa19488gut-19488.txt.txt, vol. 1 ch. 16, scholarly)
- 040.36 Figure: the French at Patay, by author: Wavrin about 6,000 before Beaugency on 17 June and 12,000 to 13,000 on the march of the 18th; Monstrelet's main body 8,000 to 9,000; the Journal has only 1,400 to 1,500 of the vanguard engaged, against more than 4,000 English. disputed. "eulz environ vi mille combatans" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Wavrin, p. 416, primary) "Si povoient estre yceulz François en tout de xn à xm mille corn- batans" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Wavrin, p. 419, primary) "amounting to.eight or nine thooaand" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 61, primary) "ne feussent que de quatorze à quinze cens combatans" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 177, primary)
- 040.37 Figure: the French losses at Patay were small. single. Wavrin, and Monstrelet's same text; no figure is given. "à peu de perte des François" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Wavrin, p. 423, primary) "without doing any great mischief to the French" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 61, primary)
- 040.38 Event: the date of Patay: Saturday 18 June 1429 in Cagny, Wavrin, the Journal, Ramsay and Lowell; Oman dates it 17 June, and Gruel's text, in error, the end of May. disputed. "Le samedi xvmfi jour duditmois de juing" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 15, primary) "The battle of Patay was won on Saturday, June 18, in the afternoon" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly) "(June 18) Battle of Patay" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, contents of ch. 27, scholarly) "it boots not to inquire (June 17, 1429)" (archive.org-download-historyofartofwa0002char-historyofartofwa0002char-djvu.txt.txt, book 14 ch. 2, scholarly) "Et fut en la fin du mois de may" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Gruel, p. 319, primary)
- 040.39 Event: where the fight was: near the village of Patay in the Beauce, about two leagues from Janville; Monstrelet puts the English half a league short of Patay, Ramsay near Coinces two or three miles short of it, and France on the edge of the Lignerolles wood between Saint-Péravy and Patay. disputed. "surent près le village de Patay" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 15, primary) "Pathai, qui sied à deux lieues de Jenville" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Wavrin, p. 424, primary) "until they came within half a league of a huge village caUed Pataye" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 61, primary) "two or three miles short of Patay" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "who was then between the hamlet of Saint-Peravy and the town of Patay" (archive.org-download-thelifeofjoanofa19488gut-19488.txt.txt, vol. 1 ch. 16, scholarly)
- 040.40 Event: the French lay that night at Patay; the fight was over about two in the afternoon, by Monstrelet. agreed for the night at Patay; single for the hour. "couchèrent audit village de Patay et aux environs" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 16, primary) "couchèrent celle nuit à Patay sur le champ" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Gruel, p. 319, primary) "which was about two o'clock in the afternoon" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 61, primary)
- 040.41 Event: the townsmen of Janville shut their gates on the English fugitives and gave the town and its great tower to the king that day; the English garrisons of the Beauce, Montpipeau, Saint-Sigismond and others, burned their places and fled. agreed. "là où les gens de la ville leur fermèrent les portes" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 177, primary) "se rendirent au roy et à ses gens … ceulx d'Yen ville" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, pp. 177-178, primary) "chastel de Yenville en Beausse" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 17, primary) "qu'ilz y boutèrent le feu, et s'en fuyrent hastivement" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 178, primary) "the inhabitants rose and barred the gates" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 10, scholarly)
- 040.42 Border: after the week of 11 to 18 June the English held no place on the Loire about Orléans: the French held the river some fifty miles above Orléans as far as Bonny after Jargeau, and after Patay Meung, Beaugency and Janville were the king's and the Beauce was clear almost to Paris. agreed. "fifty miles above Orleans ^ was controlled by the French" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 10, scholarly) "The English at once evacuated all the places they still held in the Beauce" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 10, scholarly) "trois notables places, c'est assavoir la ville et chasteau de Meun sur Laire, la ville et chasteau de Baugency et la ville et chastel de Yenville en Beausse" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 17, primary) "comme Mont-Pipeau, Sainct- Sigismont et autres places fortes" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 178, primary)
- 040.43 Event: Richemont was sent home after Patay: Alençon dared not bring him to the king, and the king bade him return to his own lands. agreed. "Le duc d'Alencon ne osa mener le conestable de\ers le roy" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 16, primary) "le roy manda à monseigneur le connestable qu'il s'en re- tournast en sa maison" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Gruel, p. 319, primary) "La Tremoille, how- ever, refused to accept of the Constable's services" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27 note, scholarly)
- 040.44 Event: on Sunday 19 June Joan and Alençon went back to Orléans with the prisoners and stayed there until Friday 24 June. agreed. "s'en alèrent gésir à Orléens et en tour la ville" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 16, primary) "On the morrow, the French returned to Orleans and the adjacent parts with their prisoners" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 61, primary)
- 040.45 Event: at Patay Alençon told the captive Talbot that he had not expected such a day that morning, and Talbot answered that it was the fortune of war. single. Alençon's deposition; Lowell follows it. "I said to Talbot that in the morning I had never expected what had happened" (archive.org-download-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-djvu.txt.txt, Alençon's deposition, primary)
- 040.46 Event: after Patay Joan dismounted to hold the head of an English prisoner struck down by his French captor and had him confessed. single. The deposition of Louis de Contes, her page; Lowell follows it. "she got down from her horse, had him confessed, supporting his head herself" (archive.org-download-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-djvu.txt.txt, deposition of Louis de Contes, primary)
- 040.47 Event: after the Loire victories the princes and captains wished the king to go into Normandy, others to take La Charité and the upper Loire first; Joan held for Reims, and the council gave way. agreed. "the nobles of the Blood Royal and the captains wished the King to go into Normandy, and not to Rheims" (archive.org-download-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-djvu.txt.txt, Dunois's deposition, primary) "qui luy desconseilloient de entre- prendre le chemin d'aler à Rains" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 17, primary) "some of his councilors urged him to reduce these towns before his de- parture" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.48 Event: Joan joined the king at Gien on Friday 24 June; impatient at the delay she went to lodge in the fields two days before he left, and the king set out from Gien on Wednesday 29 June. agreed. "Et celui jour furent au giste devers le roy en la ville de Gien sur Laire" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 17, primary) "et par despit se deslogea et ala logier aux champs deux jours avant le partement du roy" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 18, primary) "Le roy fut audit lieu de Gien jusques au mercredi" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 17, primary) "29th June Charles left Gien, Jeanne, in her impatience, preceding him by a day or two" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly)
- 040.49 Event: the king had no money to pay the army, and knights, squires and common men went with Joan unpaid. agreed. "combien que le roy n'avoit pas argent pour souldoier son armée" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 18, primary) "The royal treasury was almost empty, the pay was the scantiest" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.50 Event: at the council before Troyes the captains were told that the army lacked artillery and money, and most held Troyes too strong for it. single. The Journal du siège; Lowell follows it. "la faulte d'artillerie et d'ar- gent" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 182, primary) "spoke of the want of food and money, artillery and men" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.51 Event: Auxerre, a Burgundian town, would not open its gates: under a truce it sold food to the army and promised to do as Troyes, Châlons and Reims did, and after about three days the king passed on. agreed. "baillèrent à l'ost du roy beaucoup de vivres" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 181, primary) "le roy trois jours environ" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 181, primary) "engaged to render similar obedience" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 63, primary) "They sopplied the king's army with provision for money" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 63, primary) "Auxerre closed its gates against her" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "Auxerre supplied the hungry army with food" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.52 Event: the citizens of Auxerre paid La Trémoille 2,000 écus to spare them an assault, which Joan and several captains thought easy. single. The Journal du siège, copied by the Chronique de la Pucelle; Lang and France tell it after them, Lang as a thing said. "donnèrent seerettement deux mil escus au seigneur de la Tre- moille" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 181, primary) "La Tre*moi'lle was said to have secretly received a bribe of two thousand crowns" (archive.org-download-maidoffrancebein00languoft-maidoffrancebein00languoft-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 15, scholarly)
- 040.53 Event: Saint-Florentin submitted to the king on the road from Auxerre to Troyes, in the Journal's order and Ramsay's; Monstrelet names it before Auxerre. agreed. "s'en alla vers Sainct-Florentin , qui luyfut rendu paisible" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 181, primary) "The king on his march reduced two towns to his obedience, Gergeau and St. Florentin" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 63, primary) "but St. Florentin yielded" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly)
- 040.54 Event: Troyes refused the summons and its garrison sallied; the army lay before it some days short of food, five days in the Journal, three in Monstrelet, from Friday 8 to Sunday 10 July in Cagny. disputed. "qui se tint là ainsi comme en siège" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 182, primary) "il y en avoit de cincq a six mil qui furent près de huict jours sans menger pain" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 182, primary) "He was three days there before Ihe inhabitants would admit him as their lord" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 63, primary) "the French, in lack even of bread, were living on beans and parched corn" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly)
- 040.55 Figure: the garrison of Troyes was 500 to 600 English and Burgundians. single. The Journal du siège; the Chronique de la Pucelle has the same words. "de cinq à six cens Angloys et Bourgoignons" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 181, primary)
- 040.56 Event: the king's arrival before Troyes, by author: the advance guard on 5 July in Lowell, Joan before the town on 6 July in Ramsay, the king after dinner on Friday 8 July in Cagny. disputed. "At about nine o'clock in the morning the advance-guard of the royal army appeared, and formally summoned Troyes to sur- render" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly) "On the 6th July 4 Jeanne took up her position outside Troyes" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "Charles came on the 8th July" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27 note, scholarly) "Le roy arriva devant ledit lieu de Troye après disner, le vendredy vme jour de jullet" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 18, primary)
- 040.57 Event: at a council before Troyes the chancellor, Regnault de Chartres, archbishop of Reims, heard most of the captains advise retreat; Robert le Maçon asked that Joan be heard; she promised the town within two or three days, by love or force, and set about readying an assault, and the bishop and townsmen came out to treat. agreed. "maistre Robert le Maçon" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 182, primary) "que la cite de Troyes seroit mise en son obeyssance dedans deux ou trois jours" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 183, primary) "the Bishop x and citizens came all trembling and quaking to place their submission in the King's hands" (archive.org-download-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-djvu.txt.txt, Dunois's deposition, primary) "When it came to the turn of Robert le Magon" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.58 Event: the terms of Troyes: the garrison to go with its goods, the town a general pardon, the churchmen named under Henry VI to keep their benefices. agreed. "firent composicion que les gens de guerre s'en yroyent eulx et leurs biens, et ceulx de la ville auroient abolicion général" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 183, primary) "It was agreed that the soldiers should be allowed to retire with their property" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, citing the Recueil des Ordonnances, scholarly)
- 040.59 Event: the king entered Troyes on Sunday 10 July; Ramsay dates the capitulation 9 July. agreed for the entry. "et le dimanche xe jour se midrent en l'obéissance du roy" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 18, primary) "On Sunday, July 10, Charles entered Troyes" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly) "The King entered on the 10th" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27 note, scholarly)
- 040.60 Event: the departing garrison of Troyes would have led off its French prisoners under the terms; Joan would not allow it, and the king paid their ransoms. single. The Journal du siège; Lowell follows it with the chronicles that copy it. "les racheta le roy et en paya aucunement leurs maistres" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 184, primary) "the king was compelled to pay the captors a reasonable ransom" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.61 Person: Friar Richard, a preaching friar lately driven from Paris, followed the king's army: Lowell has him come over to Joan at Troyes, a Franciscan; Monstrelet has him with the king already on the march from Gien, of the order of St Augustine. agreed that he followed the army; by author for where he joined it. "He was always accompanied by the Maid and a pieadiing firiar" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 63, primary) "He joined himself to the royal expedi- tion and followed Joan" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.62 Event: Châlons opened to the king: its bishop and burgesses came out to him, and Cagny has him received there on Thursday 14 July. agreed. "car lévesque et les bourgoys luy vindrent au devant, et luy firent pleine obeyssance" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 184, primary) "le jeudi ensuivant fut moult honnourablement receu en la cité de Chaalons" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 18, primary) "deputies arrived from ChSdons, who brought him the keys of their town" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 64, primary) "It now eagerly opened its gates" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.63 Event: the king lodged at the archbishop's castle of Sept-Saulx, four leagues from Reims; the Anglo-Burgundian captains, Châtillon and Saveuses, left the city; the burgesses came to Sept-Saulx with the keys; and the king entered Reims on Saturday 16 July after dinner. The printed Monstrelet has Friday 6 July, a figure Quicherat says the editions of Monstrelet garble. agreed. "il se loga à quatre lieues près, à ung ehasteau nommé Sepsaulx" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 184, primary) "les seigneurs de Chastilïon sur Marne et de Saveuses" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 184, primary) "the ke3rs of the city of Bheims were presented to him" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 64, primary) "le samedi xvic jour dudit mois de jullet, après disner, Je roy arriva en la ville de Rains" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 19, primary) "yer le roy arriva en ceste ville de Bains" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, letter of three Angevin gentlemen, 17 July 1429, primary) "Charles entered the latter place on the 16th July" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "The king made his public entry into Bheims on Friday, the 6th day of July" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 64, primary) "d'erreurs de chifire introduites dans toutes les éditions de Monstrelet" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, Quicherat's note to the Angevin letter, primary)
- 040.64 Event: Charles VII was anointed and crowned in the cathedral of Reims on Sunday 17 July 1429 by Regnault de Chartres, archbishop of Reims; the service lasted from nine to two. agreed for the day and the archbishop; single for the hours. "Le dimanche xvne jour dudit mois, le roy fut sacré et couronné audit lieu de Rains" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 19, primary) "Aujourd'hui a esté sacré et couronné" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, letter of three Angevin gentlemen, primary) "Next morning, Sunday, 17th July, he was fairly" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "lequel service a duré de- puis neuf heures jusqu'à deux heures" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, letter of three Angevin gentlemen, primary)
- 040.65 Event: through the rite Joan stood beside the king holding her standard. agreed. "la Pucelle s'est tousjours tenue joignant du roy, tenant son estendart en sa main" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, letter of three Angevin gentlemen, primary) "She added that her standard was in the church at Reims; and she thought that it was fairly near the altar" (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, trial, examination of 3 March 1431, primary) "The Maid, flag in hand, stood by the altar" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "la Pucelle tenant son estendart en sa main ; laquelle en effet estoit" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Chronique de la Pucelle, variant in a note to the Journal, p. 186, primary)
- 040.66 Event: asked at her trial why her standard was borne into the church at Reims rather than other captains' standards, Joan answered that it had been in the perils and that was reason enough for it to be honoured. single. The trial record of 1431. "It had been present in the perils; that was reason enough for it to be honored" (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, trial, examination in prison, 17 March 1431, primary)
- 040.67 Event: Alençon knighted the king and stood in place of the absent Duke of Burgundy; Clermont, Vendôme, Laval and La Trémoille stood for the other lay peers, and for the peers of the Church the archbishop of Reims and the bishop of Châlons were there, with the bishops of Sées and Orléans in place of the others. agreed. "Ce jour le duc d'Alençon fist cheva- lier le rov et le servit de per de France ou lieu du duc de Bourgoigne" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 20, primary) "d'Alençon a fait le roy chevalier, et les des- susditz représentoient les pairs de France" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, letter of three Angevin gentlemen, primary) "his place was taken by the duke of Alen(jon, who knighted the king" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly)
- 040.68 Event: the Holy Ampulla was brought from the abbey of Saint-Remi by its abbot, escorted by the marshals Boussac and Rais, Graville and the admiral Culan, armed and mounted, who rode into the cathedral. agreed. "pour avoir la saincte ampole" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 185, primary) "le mareschal de Bossac, les seigneurs de Rays, Gra- ville, et l'admirai avec leurs quatre bannières" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, letter of three Angevin gentlemen, primary) "Escorted by four of the king's captains armed and mounted, the abbot rode his hackney through the great west door" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly)
- 040.69 Event: the Journal du siège has Joan kneel to the king after the anointing, weeping, and say that God's will was done, who wished her to raise the siege of Orléans and bring him to Reims; Lowell says whether she said this cannot be known. open. "Quant la Pucelle vit que le roy estoit sacré et cou- ronné, elle se agenoulla" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 186, primary) "Whether Joan said precisely this or not cannot be known" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly)
- 040.70 Event: on the day of the coronation Joan wrote to Philip, Duke of Burgundy, bidding him make peace with the king; he did not answer. agreed. "On the very day of the coronation she wrote this letter" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly) "second appeal to the Duke of Burgundy" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly)
- 040.71 Event: Burgundian envoys came to the king at Reims: as the consecration ended in Lowell, on 16 July, the eve, in Ramsay's note after Beaucourt. agreed that they came; disputed for the day. "The consecration was hardly over when his ambassa- dors reached Rheims" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly) "his envoys at Rheims induced La Tr&noille to sign a fifteen days* truce" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "The envoys joined the French at Rheims on the 16th July" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27 note, scholarly)
- 040.72 Event: at Reims the envoys got La Trémoille to sign a truce of fifteen days, and Joan was told the duke would give up Paris at its end. single. Ramsay; Lowell has only that truces were probably proposed. "his envoys at Rheims induced La Tr&noille to sign a fifteen days* truce" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly)
- 040.73 Event: Joan's father and her cousin Laxart came to Reims, and the town paid his lodging. single. Lowell, from documents in Quicherat. "cousin Laxart and her father, who had come to see her triumph" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.74 Event: Christine de Pizan finished her Ditié of Joan on the last day of July 1429. single. The poem's own closing lines; Ramsay prints it from the same edition. "Donné ce ditié par Christine" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, Christine de Pizan, Ditié, stanza 61, primary) "Et vingt et neuf, le jour où fine Le mois de juillet" (archive.org-download-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-ProcesDeCondamnationV5-djvu.txt.txt, Christine de Pizan, Ditié, stanza 61, primary)
- 040.75 Event: from Reims the king went to the shrine of Saint-Marcoul at Corbeny and touched for the King's Evil, as kings did after their anointing. agreed. "aprez s'en ala (1) à Sainct-Marcoul" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 187, primary) "en l'abbaye de Saint Marcoul" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 20, primary) "pilgrimage to Coibeni, to pay adoration to St Maroou" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 64, primary) "he touched for the King's Evil" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly)
- 040.76 Event: the day the king left Reims for Corbeny: Thursday 21 July in Cagny, Lowell and Ramsay; 20 July in France and by the Journal's count of the coronation day and two days after. disputed. "Le roy fut audit lieu de Rains jusques au jeudi ensuivant" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 20, primary) "Thursday, July 21, Cliarles rode to the abbey of St. Marcoul" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly) "On the aist July Charles left" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "departed from the city on the 20th of July and went to Saint-Marcoul-de-Corbeny" (archive.org-download-thelifeofjoanofa19488gut-19488.txt.txt, vol. 1 ch. 18, scholarly) "séjourna le roy à Reims, et aprez s'en ala" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 187, primary)
- 040.77 Event: Laon submitted: its keys or its deputies were brought to the king at Saint-Marcoul de Corbeny. agreed. "auquel lieu furent aportés au roy les clefs de la cité de Lan" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 20, primary) "Thither came deputiaB firom Lub, to submit themselTes to his obedience" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 64, primary) "At Corbeny he received the submission of the town of Laon" (archive.org-download-thelifeofjoanofa19488gut-19488.txt.txt, vol. 1 ch. 18, scholarly) "Déjà Laon Iui a fait connaître sa soumission" (archive.org-rt-histoire-de-charles-vii-v-2-Beaucourt-20Histoire-20de-20Charles-20VII-20v-202-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 5, p. 234, scholarly)
- 040.78 Event: the king went on by Vailly on 22 July and entered Soissons, which had sent him its keys, on Saturday 23 July. agreed. "le samedi xxine jour dudit mois, le roy vint disner, souper et gésir en la cité de Soissons" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 20, primary) "s'en vint à une petite ville fermée, nommée Vailly" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 187, primary) "having received the keys of Soissons, he entered that city on Saturday, July 23" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly) "on the aand he was at Vailly" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly)
- 040.79 Event: Château-Thierry, held by the Burgundian lords of Châtillon, Croy and Brimeu, yielded when its townsmen leaned to the king; the garrison marched off with its goods to Bedford in Paris, and the king entered on the evening of 29 July and stayed until 1 August. agreed. "yielded up the town and cartle to king Charies, and marched away with tbeir effects ssd baggage undisturbed" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 64, primary) "Having quitted Soissons, the royal army reached Chateau-Thierry on the 29th" (archive.org-download-thelifeofjoanofa19488gut-19488.txt.txt, vol. 2 ch. 1, scholarly) "He stayed at Chateau Thierry from July 29 to August i" (archive.org-download-maidoffrancebein00languoft-maidoffrancebein00languoft-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 16, scholarly)
- 040.80 Event: the king reached Provins on Tuesday 2 August 1429 and stayed until Friday 5 August, by Cagny. agreed for the day he came. "vint à giste en la ville de Provins" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 21, primary) "Et y séjourna jusques au vendredi V jour ensuivant" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Cagny, p. 21, primary) "it proceeded to Provins, which was reached on August 2" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly) "On August 2 he was at Provins" (archive.org-download-maidoffrancebein00languoft-maidoffrancebein00languoft-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 16, scholarly) "On the morrow he gained Provins" (archive.org-download-thelifeofjoanofa19488gut-19488.txt.txt, vol. 2 ch. 1, scholarly)
- 040.81 Border: the towns that came over to Charles VII after the coronation: Laon, Soissons, Château-Thierry, Crécy, Coulommiers and Provins, given as a list of towns. agreed. "Laon, ChAteau Thierry, Coulommiers, Provins, hastened to offer their allegiance" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "oissons ouvre ses portes; Château-Thierry, Crécy, Coulom- iniers, Provins se déclarent Français" (archive.org-rt-histoire-de-charles-vii-v-2-Beaucourt-20Histoire-20de-20Charles-20VII-20v-202-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 5, p. 235, scholarly) "Coulommiers, Crecy-en-Brie, and Provins submitted" (archive.org-download-thelifeofjoanofa19488gut-19488.txt.txt, vol. 2 ch. 1, scholarly)
- 040.82 Event: from Soissons, with the road to Paris open, the army turned south by Château-Thierry to Provins and did not march on Paris; Ramsay lays it to La Trémoille, Beaucourt to the wish to spare the Duke of Burgundy while the two treated. agreed. "La Tr&noille preferred to lead him southwards to ChAteau- Thierry and Provins" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "au lieu de marcher sur Paris par Com- piègne et Senlis, on se dirige vers Château-Thierry et Provins" (archive.org-rt-histoire-de-charles-vii-v-2-Beaucourt-20Histoire-20de-20Charles-20VII-20v-202-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 5, p. 235, scholarly) "did not take the direct road to Paris, but went almost due south to Chateau Thierry" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, scholarly)
- 040.83 Event: on 31 July, at Château-Thierry, the king exempted Domrémy and Greux from taxes at Joan's request. single. The grant in Quicherat 5, as both Lang and Lowell cite it. "he granted remission of taxation to her native villages, Domremy and Greux" (archive.org-download-maidoffrancebein00languoft-maidoffrancebein00languoft-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 16, scholarly) "Charles forever exempted the people of Domremy and Greux from all taxes" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly)
- 040.84 Figure: the reinforcement Cardinal Beaufort brought Bedford in July 1429, by author: Ramsay 250 spears and 2,500 bows entering Paris on 25 July; Monstrelet about 4,000 combatants. disputed. "the 25th July 250 spears and 2500 bows" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, scholarly) "about four thousand combatants had been sent from Kngjawl" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 62, primary)
- 040.85 Border: Champagne in June 1429: its towns had held for the Anglo-Burgundians since about the treaty of Troyes; Lowell gives them small garrisons under captains in English pay, Oman says Bedford had left them to themselves without English garrisons. disputed. "Most of them had fallen to the Anglo-Burgundians about the time of the" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly) "there were garrisons in most of these places, commanded by captains in the pay of the English" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 11, scholarly) "had been mainly trusted to themselves by the regent Bedford, and were without English garrisons" (archive.org-download-historyofartofwa0002char-historyofartofwa0002char-djvu.txt.txt, book 14 ch. 2, scholarly)
- 040.86 Border: Bedford's own memorandum, of December 1433 as Lang dates it, lists the cities that went over after Orléans without resistance: Reims, Troyes, Châlons, Laon, Sens, Provins, Senlis, Lagny, Creil, Beauvais, with most of Champagne, the Beauce and part of Picardy. single. The memorandum, quoted by Lang. "cities and towns, as Reims, Troyes, Chalons, Laon, Sens, Provins,!" (archive.org-download-maidoffrancebein00languoft-maidoffrancebein00languoft-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 17, quoting Bedford's memorandum, scholarly) "Bedford's paper is really of December 1433" (archive.org-download-maidoffrancebein00languoft-maidoffrancebein00languoft-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 17, scholarly)
- 040.87 Event: the army went from Selles to Orléans, where Joan came a second time on 9 June by the town's accounts, and on Saturday 11 June marched out of Orléans for Jargeau, twelve miles off by Lowell's reckoning. agreed. "ilz s'en partirent le samedy unziesme jour de juing" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 170, primary) "La deuxième entrée de la Pucelle à Orléans eut lieu le 9 juin , d'après les comptes de dépense de la ville" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Quicherat's note to the Journal du siège, p. 170, primary) "started to travel along the twelve miles" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 9, scholarly) "There were three thousand soldiers or there- abouts, who had come to Orleans with Joan" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 9, scholarly)

# Research for Joan of Arc

## 050 paris-and-compiegne

Coverage: primary (Perceval de Cagny's Chroniques, the household chronicle of Alençon; the Journal d'un bourgeois de Paris, from inside the walls; Monstrelet's Chronicles in Johnes's translation, book 2 ch. 66-86, Monstrelet being present at Compiègne; Chastellain and Jean Chartier and the Journal du siège in Quicherat 4; the letters of November 1429 to Clermont and Riom and the Duke of Burgundy's letter of 23 May 1430 in Quicherat 5; her own answers in the trial of 1431 in Barrett's translation; d'Aulon's and Thierry's depositions of 1456 in Murray) and scholarly (Lowell ch. 12-16, Lang ch. 16-20, Ramsay 1 ch. 27-28, Beaucourt 2 ch. 6, Champion's sketches in Barrett). The Bray check, Montépilloy and the attack on Paris have Cagny and the Burgundian chroniclers on each side; Saint-Pierre-le-Moûtier rests on d'Aulon alone for its course, with the letters of 7 and 9 November for its taking; La Charité has Cagny, the Berry herald and Jean Chartier in Quicherat 4, the letters, the Bourges levy of 24 November and the three lives; the Berry herald gives its length (about a month), an assault and the loss of the artillery, and no source read gives the day it was raised; Compiègne has Monstrelet and Chastellain from the Burgundian camp, Cagny, the Duke's letter and her own account. No source read gives a plan of the walls of Paris, the defenders' posts, the number of the garrison of La Charité or the losses at Saint-Pierre. The Wikipedia articles on Montépilloy (a commune stub), the siege of Paris, Saint-Pierre and La Charité give dates and figures that no source that counts carries (see 050.3, 050.21, 050.37, 050.41). France's Life (vol. 2 ch. 1-7) was not needed beyond these and was not quoted. No work by Pernoud, DeVries or Barker could be read; the Wikipedia article on the siege of Compiègne cites Pernoud and Clin and DeVries.

- 050.1 Event: early August 1429, the royal council at Provins resolves to cross the Seine at Bray and go back over the Loire; the townsmen of Bray promise the passage, but the night before the crossing an Anglo-Burgundian force occupies Bray, and the captains of the war party, glad of the check, turn the army north again toward Crépy and Paris. single (Jean Chartier and the Journal du siège, whose texts run close here; Lowell and Ramsay follow them). "pour aller à une ville nommée Bray sur Saine, assez prez de Provins, pour s'en aler avec son armée en son pays obéissant oultre la rivière de Loire" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Jean Chartier, p. 79, primary) "mirent en leur ville grant compaignie d'Anglois et Bourguignons, le soir devant qu'il y devoit passer" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 188, primary) "an Anglo -Burgundian force seized Bray and held it so strongly that a battle was needed to force the passage of the Seine" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 165, scholarly) "the bridge over the Seine at Bray was still open, turned in that direction, only to find that the place had just been occupied by an Anglo- Burgundian force" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 402, scholarly)
- 050.2 Person: John, Duke of Bedford, regent of France for Henry VI, leads the Anglo-Burgundian army against Charles in August 1429; from Montereau on 7 August he sends Charles a letter of challenge, which reaches him at Crépy on 11 August. agreed. "On the 7th August Bedford was at Montereau, and sent a challenge to ( Charles of Valois'" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 403, scholarly) "On August 11 Bedford's letter was received by Charles at Crepy-en-Valois" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 167, scholarly) "Le duc de Bethford, les capitaines englois, acompaigniez de viij à ix mille Englois" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 162, primary) "Given at Montereau-fiiut-Ycmne the 7ih day of August" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 65, the letter of Bedford, primary)
- 050.3 Event: Montépilloy, Sunday 14 and Monday 15 August 1429: the king's army and Bedford's face each other between Montépilloy and Senlis; after a skirmish on the evening of the 14th they skirmish all day on the 15th, Bedford keeps to his entrenched camp, and no battle is fought; on the morning of the 16th Bedford falls back by Senlis to Paris and the French go back to the king at Crépy. agreed (Cagny, Monstrelet's "two days", Lowell, Ramsay, Lang, Beaucourt). The Wikipedia article on the siege of Paris dates "the battle of Montépilloy" 26 August in a sentence with no footnote, against every source read. "Le dimenche, xiiij jour du mois d'aoust ensuivant" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 161, primary) "Le lundy xv jour dudit mois d'aoust" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 162, primary) "nouvelles leur vindrent que les Englois retournoient à Senlis et droit à Paris" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 164, primary) "ever prepared to engage, for the apace of two days" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 66, primary) "On Sunday, August 14, the two armies came face to face at Montepilloy, not far from Senlis." (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 167, scholarly) "On the 15th August the two armies faced each other all" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 403, scholarly) "Tout se borna à des esrarmouches" (archive.org-rt-histoire-de-charles-vii-v-2-Beaucourt-20Histoire-20de-20Charles-20VII-20v-202-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 6, p. 237, scholarly)
- 050.4 Event: the ground and the two armies at Montépilloy: the French lodge by a hedge in the fields near Montépilloy, about two leagues from Senlis; Bedford lies half a league from Senlis, between them and the town, by the abbey of Notre-Dame-de-la-Victoire on the little river Nonette, fortified overnight with stakes, ditches and carts, the river at his back; the English are on one wing and the Picards and French of the Burgundian party on the other (the right, Lowell); the French form an advance guard (Alençon), a centre (René of Bar), a rear with the king and La Trémoille, and skirmishers under Joan, the Bastard and La Hire (Lowell). agreed for the two camps; single (Lowell) for the order of the French divisions. "logiés à une haye aux champs près Montpillour*, environ deux lieues près la cité de Senlis" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 162, primary) "estoient logiés à demye lieue près de Senlis, entre noz gens et ladite ville" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 162, primary) "toute la nuit se fortiffierent de paux, de fossez et de leur charrey audevant d'eulx, et la rivière les fortifiet par desriere" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 163, primary) "The duke of Bedford chose a strong post, well strengthened, on the rear and wings, with thick hedge- rows." (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 66, primary) "His main body was English, his right wing composed of Picards sent to him by Philip of Burgundy." (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 167, scholarly) "The French army was formed in four divisions … under Joan, the Bastard, and La Hire" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 167, scholarly) "Next day they took up a position on the Nonette, near Notre Dame de la Victoire, outside Senlis" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 403, scholarly)
- 050.5 Figure: the strengths at Montépilloy. disputed: Cagny gives Bedford 8,000 to 9,000 English and the French 6,000 to 7,000 (Lang follows him); Monstrelet gives the king men-at-arms much superior in number to the English, with 600 to 800 Burgundians in Bedford's army; the Journal du siège says Bedford left Paris with more than 10,000 and had as many men as the king; Lowell and Ramsay make the French the more numerous. "acompaigniez de viij à ix mille Englois" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 162, primary) "with some 6000 men, passed the night at Montepilloy. The English are reckoned at from 8000 to 9000." (archive.org-download-maidoffrancebein00languoft-maidoffrancebein00languoft-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 16, p. 195, scholarly) "The king had a force of men-at-arma with him nrach sapenor ii nnmbera to the English" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 66, primary) "from six to eight hundred combatants from the duke of Burgundy" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 66, primary) "combien qu'il avoit bien autant de gens que le roy" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 188, primary) "Although the French were more numerous than the English" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 167, scholarly)
- 050.6 Figure: the losses at Montépilloy, about 300 killed in the skirmishes, side not known, Monstrelet; Cagny has the captain of Orbec and ten or twelve English killed on the evening of the 14th. single. "about three hundred men were killed in these different skirmishes" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 66, primary) "y fut mort du costé des Englois le capitaine d'Orbec et x ou xij autres" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 162, primary)
- 050.7 Event: Compiègne sends its keys to the king at Crépy on Wednesday 17 August 1429 and he enters it on Thursday 18 August; Guillaume de Flavy is made its captain, at the townsmen's asking, on the day of the entry. agreed. "Le mecredi xvij jour dudit mois furent aportés devers le roy les clefs de la ville de Gompiegne" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 164, primary) "He lost no time in going thither, and was received by the inhabitants with great joy, and lodged in the royal palace." (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 69, primary) "on Thursday, August 18, proceeded to Compiegne" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 169, scholarly) "Par epecial en feist capitaine ung vaillant gentilhomme du pays de Picardie, appelle Guillaume deFlavy" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 196, primary) "Il fut agréé toutefois le 18 août 1429, jour même de l'entrée du roi" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, Quicherat's note, p. 196, primary)
- 050.8 Event: Senlis and Beauvais come over to Charles in the second half of August 1429: Senlis yields by treaty to Vendôme and the marshals sent from Crépy (letters of abolition dated 22 August, Tuetey), and Beauvais drives out its bishop, Pierre Cauchon. agreed. "ilz se rendirent au roy et à la Pucelle. Le conte de Vendosme demoura gouverneur et garde de la place" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 164, primary) "se rendit aux Arminalx la cité de Beauvays et la cité de Senlis" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 515, primary) "lettres d'abolition datées du 22 août 1429" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, Tuetey's note 922, primary) "Senlis surrendered on capitulation to the king" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 70, primary) "vais had driven out Cauchon, its bishop count, a violent Anglo-Burgundian" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 13, p. 171, scholarly) "Expelled from Beauvais with the English (August, 1429)" (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, Champion's sketch of Pierre Cauchon, scholarly)
- 050.9 Border: August 1429, the towns between the Oise and Paris that came over to Charles, as the sources list them: Compiègne, Senlis, Beauvais, Creil, Pont-Sainte-Maxence, Choisy, Gournay-sur-Aronde and others (Monstrelet); Senlis, Creil, Pont-Sainte-Maxence, Beauvais, Aumale and Blangy (Ramsay); Lagny on the Marne (Lowell); Paris and Normandy stay with Henry VI. Given as lists of towns, not as a line. agreed. "namely, Creil, Beauvais, Choisy, le Pontde St Maixoe." (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 70, primary) "During the conferences Senlis, Creil, Pont-Sainte-Maxence, Beauvais, Aumdle, and Blangy changed hands." (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 404, scholarly) "in the preceding August had acknowledged Charles" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 15, p. 202, scholarly)
- 050.10 Event: on 28 August 1429 a truce between Charles VII and Philip of Burgundy is signed at Compiègne. agreed for the date; disputed for the terms: Lowell has it begin at once and run to Christmas over the country north of the Seine from Nogent to the sea, the river towns excepted, with Philip free to relieve Paris; Ramsay has it run from 28 September to Christmas over the left bank from Nogent to Harfleur, Paris and the river towns excluded; Beaucourt calls it a truce of four months. Monstrelet has the truce run to Easter by the time the king went south. "a general truce, which was signed on August 28. It covered all the country north of the Seine from Nogent, sixty miles above Paris, to the sea, except the cities and fortresses on the river at which it could be crossed." (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 170, scholarly) "a futile truce was signed on the 28th August The truce was to run only from 28th September to Christmas, and was to cover only the territory on the left bank of the Seine, from Nogent to Harfleur ; Paris, and all towns actually situate on the river, being excluded." (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 404, scholarly) "un traité préliminaire portant trève pendant quatre mois fût signé à Compiègne le 28 août" (archive.org-rt-histoire-de-charles-vii-v-2-Beaucourt-20Histoire-20de-20Charles-20VII-20v-202-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 6, p. 236, scholarly) "for the truce between the Burgundians and French did not expire until Easter" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 72, primary)
- 050.11 Person: Regnault of Chartres, Archbishop of Reims and chancellor, heads the embassy to Philip at Arras in August 1429 and with La Trémoille and Gaucourt governs the king and his war; after Reims he stands for peace with Burgundy in the council (Champion). agreed. "The principal persons of this emldsr were, the archbiBhop of Rheims" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 67, primary) "le seigneur de la Trimoille, le sire de Gaucourt, qui lors gouvernoient le corps du roy et le fait de sa guerre" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 171, primary) "Regnault, after Reims, always represented the cause of peace in the King's council" (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, Champion's sketch of Regnault de Chartres, scholarly)
- 050.12 Event: after Montépilloy and the loss of Senlis, Bedford leaves Paris to the Burgundians and to Louis of Luxembourg, bishop of Thérouanne, Henry VI's chancellor, with L'Isle-Adam, and goes to Normandy. agreed. "il lessa Paris ou gouvernement des bourgois, du sire de l'Ille Adam^ et des Bourguignons de sa compaignie" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 165, primary) "délaissant a Paris messire Loys de Luxemhoug, évesque de Therouenne, soy disant chancelier de France pour le roy Henry" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 197, primary) "he divided his forces, leaving Paris to the Burgundian Bishop of Therouanne" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 404, scholarly)
- 050.13 Person: Philip the Good, Duke of Burgundy, accepts from Bedford the office of governor of Paris during the talks of August 1429. single (Lowell, citing Beaucourt). "accepting from Bedford the office of governor of Paris" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 170, scholarly)
- 050.14 Event: on Tuesday 23 August Joan and Alençon leave the king at Compiègne with a strong company and take Saint-Denis on 25 August (the Bourgeois, Lowell) or lodge there on Friday 26 August (Cagny); for a week and more they skirmish at the gates of Paris. disputed for the day, 25 or 26 August. "le mardy xxiij jour dudit mois d'aoust, la Pucelle et le duc d'Alençon partirent dudit lieu de Compiegne" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 165, primary) "le vendredi ensuivant, xxvj jour dudit mois, furent la Pucelle, le duc d'Alençon" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 165, primary) "le XXVe jour d'aoust, fut prinse par eulx la ville de Sainct-Denis" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 516, primary) "On the 25th they took St. Denis" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 12, p. 169, scholarly) "par chacun jour deux ou trois foiz nos gens estoient à l'escharmouche aux portes de Paris" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 166, primary)
- 050.15 Event: Alençon goes to the king at Senlis on 1 and 5 September, and the king comes to Saint-Denis on Wednesday 7 September; Joan and the captains lodge that evening at La Chapelle, between Saint-Denis and Paris. agreed. "ledit d'Alençon ala devers lui le premier jour de septembre" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 166, primary) "le mecredi fut à disner audit lieu de Saint Denys" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 166, primary) "He reached Saint-Denis on the 7th September" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 405, scholarly) "s'en vindrent loger en un village dit La Chappelle, qui est ou chemin et comme au millieu de Paris et Sainct-Denis" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 198, primary)
- 050.16 Event: the attack on Paris, Thursday 8 September 1429, the feast of the Nativity of Our Lady: the army marches from La Chapelle about eight; Joan, Marshal de Rais and Gaucourt attack the Porte Saint-Honoré from the pig market (Marché aux Pourceaux), the barrier and boulevard before the gate are fired and taken, and Alençon (with Bourbon, the Journal du siège; with Boussac, Ramsay) covers them behind a hill against a sally from the Porte Saint-Denis; the Bourgeois has the assault between the Porte Saint-Honoré and the Porte Saint-Denis, from between eleven and noon. agreed. "Le jeudi MCCGCXXix, jour de Nostre Dame, viij jour du mois de septembre" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 166, primary) "alerent donner l'assault à la porte de Saint Honnouré" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 167, primary) "le jour de la Saincte Nativité Nostre-Dame firent conjuracion, tous d'un accord, de cellui jour assaillir Paris" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 519, primary) "commencerent à assaillir entre la porte Sainct-Honoré et la porte Sainct-Denis" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 519, primary) "s'en vindrent mettre en belle ordonnance ou Marché-aux-pourceaulx, devant la porte Sainct-Honnoré" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 198, primary) "on the day of the Festival of the Nativity of Our Lady, called together all the soldiers of Charles's army to march to the attack upon Paris" (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, the seventy articles, art. 57, primary) "while Jeanne, Marshal de Rais, and the Sire de Gaucourt attacked the Porte Saint-Honor^" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 405, scholarly) "L'attaque, dirigée par Jeanne le 8 septembre, demeura infructueuse" (archive.org-rt-histoire-de-charles-vii-v-2-Beaucourt-20Histoire-20de-20Charles-20VII-20v-202-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 6, p. 238, scholarly)
- 050.17 Event: Monstrelet sets the assault on Monday the 12th of the month, from ten o'clock for four or five hours, the king himself leading the army from between Montmartre and Paris. single (Monstrelet alone; Cagny, the Bourgeois and the trial give the feast day, Thursday the 8th). "It was at length determined that on Monday, the 12th day of the month" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 70, primary)
- 050.18 Event: the moat stops the assault: past the first ditch and the ridge between the two (the dos d'âne), the second ditch is full of water too deep to cross; Joan sounds it with her lance and has faggots and wood thrown in, but it cannot be filled before night. agreed. "monta le dos dasne, duquel elle descendit jusques ou second fossé" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 199, primary) "tous chargez de grans bourées à trois hars pour emplir les fossez de Paris" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 519, primary) "the water in the fosse was so deep that they could not get at the walls" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 13, p. 176, scholarly) "the King's officers had not calculated the depth of water in the inner moat" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 405, scholarly)
- 050.19 Event: Joan is wounded by a crossbow bolt through the thigh (Cagny, the Journal du siège) or the leg (the Bourgeois, Lowell), late in the day; her standard-bearer is shot dead; she will not leave the ditch until Gaucourt and others (Cagny) or Alençon (the Journal du siège) have her carried back to La Chapelle, saying the place would have been taken; at her trial she said she was wounded before Paris and recovered in five days. agreed. "après solleil couchant , la Pucelle fut férue d'un trait de haussepié … par une cuisse" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 167, primary) "qu'ung arbalestier de Paris luy perça la cuisse d'ung traict" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 199, primary) "Et traict de son arbaleste droit à elle et lui perce la jambe tout oultre" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 519, primary) "she was wounded in the trenches before Paris, after she left Saint-Denis; but recovered in five days" (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, the public examination of 22 February, primary) "le sire de Gaucourt et autres vindrent prendre la Pucelle" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 168, primary) "le duc d'Alençon l'envoya querre , et la feist. retraire" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 199, primary) "Par mon martin, la place eust esté prinse" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 168, primary)
- 050.20 Figure: the strength of the attackers at Paris, 12,000 or more, the Bourgeois. single. "Et s'assemblerent bien XII mil ou plus" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 519, primary)
- 050.21 Figure: the defenders of Paris. disputed: Monstrelet has 400 combatants sent by the Duke of Burgundy with Louis of Luxembourg, L'Isle-Adam, Créqui and others; the Journal du siège has 2,000 combatants left by Bedford with Louis of Luxembourg, Sir John Ratcliffe and Simon Morhier; the Bourgeois says there were hardly any men-at-arms in the assault but some 40 or 50 English. The Wikipedia infobox's 3,000 English has no source that counts. "with four hundsed ccmibatants" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 70, primary) "deux mil combatans pour la garde" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 197, primary) "n'avoit aussi comme nulz hommes d'armes que environ XL ou L Anglois" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 519, primary)
- 050.22 Figure: the attackers' losses at Paris. disputed: the Bourgeois has the French herald who came for the dead swear to the captain of Paris that 1,500 were wounded, 500 or more of them dead or mortally wounded; Cagny says very few were hurt and no man died of it; Monstrelet has many killed and wounded by the guns. "lequel jura qu'ilz estoient bien quinze cens, dont bien Vc ou plus estoient mors ou navrez à mort" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 519, primary) "oncques home n'en mourut" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 167, primary) "Many of the French were driven back into the ditches" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 70, primary)
- 050.23 Event: on Friday 9 September Joan asks Alençon to sound the trumpets and return to the walls; the baron of Montmorency comes out of Paris with 50 or 60 gentlemen to join her; René, Duke of Bar, and the Count of Clermont then come from the king at Saint-Denis and bid her and the captains return to him, and the army goes back to Saint-Denis. agreed for the return to Saint-Denis; single (Cagny; Lowell and Ramsay follow him) for the order brought by Bar and Clermont and for Montmorency. "le baron de Mommorancy, qui tousjours avoit tenu le parti contraire du roy, vint de dedens la ville" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 168, primary) "vindrent le duc de Bar et le conte de Gleremont de par le roy, qui estoit à Saint Denys" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 168, primary) "Et le lendemain s'en retournèrent tous à" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 199, primary) "to renew the assault, and the King's immediate return to" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 405, scholarly)
- 050.24 Event: on the night of 9 to 10 September the bridge Alençon had thrown over the Seine near Saint-Denis is broken up, by the king's order (Cagny) or by some of the council without Alençon's knowledge (Lowell), and the crossing to attack Paris from the other side is given up. single (Cagny; Lowell follows). "toute la nuit fist dépecer ledit pont" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 169, primary) "on Friday night some of the council caused the bridge to be broken without the duke's knowledge" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 13, p. 177, scholarly)
- 050.25 Border: September 1429, what Charles keeps north of the Seine as he goes south: Charles de Bourbon, Count of Clermont, governor of the Île-de-France and the Beauvaisis, the chancellor at Beauvais, Vendôme at Senlis, Guillaume de Flavy at Compiègne, Jacques de Chabannes at Creil, Ambroise de Loré at Lagny; the commission was named on 7 September, before the assault. agreed. "Charles de Bourbon, count of Clermont, governor in chief of the Isle de France" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 72, primary) "the count de Yendome at Senlis, Willia^ de Flavy, at Compile, sir James de Chabanncs at Creil" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 72, primary) "ordonna cappitaine messire Ambroys de Loïc" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 200, primary) "The governors to rule in the North of France after Charles' return to the South were appointed on the 7th September" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 406 n., scholarly)
- 050.26 Battle: the attack on Paris, 8 September 1429 (card): Charles VII's army under Joan, Alençon, Rais, Gaucourt and Boussac against Paris, held for Henry VI by its garrison and townsmen under Louis of Luxembourg and L'Isle-Adam; the assault fails at the water-filled moat. The positions and movements of the attackers are in Cagny (1429, p. 166-169), the Journal du siège (Quicherat 4, p. 197-200) and Lowell ch. 13; the Bourgeois tells it from within the walls (§ 519); no source read gives the defenders' posts or a plan of the walls. agreed. "et ainssi faillit l'assault" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 168, primary) "mais pou y conquesterent, se ne fu douleur, honte et meschef" (archive.org-download-journaldunbourge54182gut-54182-0.txt.txt, § 519, primary) "that it was impossible to gain &e town by force against so obstinate a defence" (archive.org-download-chroniclesengue06johngoog-chroniclesengue06johngoog-djvu.txt.txt, book 2 ch. 70, primary)
- 050.27 Event: before leaving Saint-Denis, Joan offers her whole armour before the image of Our Lady and the relics of the abbey of Saint-Denis; at her trial she said it was a white harness with a sword won before Paris, offered as soldiers did when wounded, and to Saint Denis because it was the war cry of France. agreed. "elle donna et lessa tout son bernois complect devant l'image de Nostre Dame et les relicques de l'abbaye de Saint Denis" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 170, primary) "she offered a whole suit of white armor, in French “un blanc harnoys,”" (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, the examination of 17 March, primary) "because she had been wounded before Paris, she offered them to St. Denis, because it was the warcry of France" (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, the examination of 17 March, primary) "She hung up her armour at Saint-Denis and submitted." (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 405, scholarly)
- 050.28 Event: by her own account at the trial, her voice told her to stay at Saint-Denis and the lords took her away against her will. single (her answer of 22 February 1431, the court's record). "The voice told her to remain at Saint-Denis in France, and the said Jeanne wished to remain; but against her will the lords took her away." (sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu-basis-joanofarc-trial.asp.txt, the public examination of 22 February, primary)
- 050.29 Event: the king leaves Saint-Denis after dinner on Tuesday 13 September 1429 and goes back by Lagny on the Marne, Provins, Bray on the Seine (which yields to him), a ford of the Yonne below Sens, Courtenay and Montargis to Gien, where he dines on Wednesday 21 September. agreed for the route; disputed for the day he left Saint-Denis, Tuesday 13 September (Cagny, Ramsay) or Monday 12 September (the Journal du siège). "Cedit mardi xiij jour dudit mois de septembre" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 169, primary) "se partist ledouziesme jour de septembre" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 200, primary) "le mecredi xxj jour dudit mois, à disner à Gien sur Laire" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 170, primary) "s'en ala à Laigny sur Marne" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 200, primary) "et de là à Bray sur Seine" (archive.org-download-procsdecondam04joanuoft-procsdecondam04joanuoft-djvu.txt.txt, Journal du siège, p. 200, primary) "The Marne was crossed at Lagny, the Seine at Bray, and the Yonne near Sens." (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 13, p. 179 n., scholarly) "the 13th September the army moved to Lagny; next day or so they crossed the Seine at Bray. On the 21st they were disbanded at Gien" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 406, scholarly)
- 050.30 Event: at Gien, about 21 September 1429, the army that went to Reims is disbanded; Alençon goes home to his wife and his viscounty of Beaumont and the captains to their frontiers; his request to take Joan with him into Normandy (toward the marches of Brittany and Maine, Cagny; into Maine, Ramsay) is refused by Regnault of Chartres, La Trémoille and Gaucourt, and he never serves with her again. agreed. "Et ainssi fut le vouloir de la Pucelle et l'armée du roy rompue" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 170, primary) "ledit d'Alençon s'en ala devers sa femme et en sa viconté de Beaumont, et les autres capitaines chacun en sa frontière" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 170, primary) "ne vouldrent oncques consentir, ne faire, ne souffrir que la Pucelle et le duc d'Alençon fussent ensemble" (archive.org-download-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-chroniquesdeperc00cagnuoft-djvu.txt.txt, 1429, p. 171, primary) "The duke went in disgust to his estates and stayed there, never again to serve with Joan." (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 14, p. 181, scholarly) "On the 21st they were disbanded at Gien" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 27, p. 406, scholarly) "Alencon was forbidden to take her into Maine" (archive.org-download-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-lancasterandyor00ramsgoog-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 28, p. 416 n., scholarly)
- 050.31 Event: in October 1429 Joan stays about three weeks at Bourges in the house of Marguerite La Touroulde while the expedition is made ready. single (La Touroulde's deposition, which Lowell and Beaucourt follow). "from Selles she was sent on to stay with Margaret in her house at Bourges" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 14, p. 181, scholarly) "se rendit à Bourges, où, pendant les préparatifs de l'expédition, elle fut logée chez Marguerite La Touroulde" (archive.org-rt-histoire-de-charles-vii-v-2-Beaucourt-20Histoire-20de-20Charles-20VII-20v-202-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 6, p. 239, scholarly)
- 050.32 Person: Charles d'Albret, half-brother of La Trémoille (his mother's son), lieutenant-general in Berry, leads the expedition against Saint-Pierre-le-Moûtier and La Charité with Joan. agreed. "Charles of Albret was his own half-brother" (archive.org-download-joanofarc00lowe-joanofarc00lowe-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 14, p. 184, scholarly) "en compagnie du sire d'Albret (rère utérin du ministre)" (archive.org-rt-histoire-de-charles-vii-v-2-Beaucourt-20Histoire-20de-20Charles-20VII-20v-202-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 6, p. 239, scholarly) "with his half-brother, d'Albret, to attack St. Pierre le Moustier" (archive.org-download-maidoffrancebein00languoft-maidoffrancebein00languoft-djvu.txt.txt, ch. 18, p. 213, scholarly) "with a certain number of men-at-arms, of whom Lord d'Elbret was the head" (archive.org-download-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-jeannedarcmaidof028754mbp-djvu.txt.txt, testimony of d'Aulon, p. 318, primary)
- 050.33 Person: Perrinet Gressart, a soldier of fortune in Burgundy's service, captain of La Charité since he took it from the French in 1423 and holder of the towns round it; Saint-Pierre-le-Moûtier, taken by the Burgundians in 1422, has Francis of Surienne, his niece's husband, for bailiff. agreed for Gressart; single (Lowell) for Surienne and the year 1422.
